=== NOTES 6:5 | Overview and data: Andhra Pradesh - government decisions, new schemes, appointments and administrative changes
## SUB 0 | Employees, Acts and State Institutions (2017-2022 Decisions)
[5:0:0] # Government employees
[5:0:1] - **Retirement age:** raised **60 → 62** for State government employees – announced **7 January 2022** with the 11th PRC (**23% fitment**); ordinance of 31 Jan 2022, effective **1 January 2022** (earlier 58 → 60 in 2014).
[5:0:2] - **APSRTC merger:** the **AP State Road Transport Corporation (Absorption of Employees into Government Service) Act, 2019** (Act 36 of 2019; Bill passed 16 Dec 2019) absorbed about **51,500 RTC staff** into a new **Public Transport Department** from **1 January 2020**, on par with government employees.
[5:0:3] # State emblem (order of 14 November 2018)
[5:0:4] TABLE head: Version | Authorised users
  [5:0:4.0] Multi-coloured | CM, Ministers, Chief Secretary, Secretaries to Government, Advocate General, all Heads of Departments, District Collectors and Magistrates
  [5:0:4.1] Blue | District HoDs and mid-level Secretariat officers
[5:0:5] - **Not authorised:** **heads of autonomous bodies** (APPSC Divisional Accounts Officer key).
[5:0:6] # Endowments Act (Act 30 of 1987)
[5:0:7] - **Executive Officer, Sec. 29(3):** custody of **records, accounts, jewels**, valuables and funds; **collection of income** and incurring expenditure; sues and is sued; deposits money in bank/treasury; emergency works for the **safety of pilgrims**.
[5:0:8] - **Not a duty:** '**publication of institutions**' (APPSC Endowment Prelims 2022).
[5:0:9] # Institutions and policies
[5:0:10] - **APHRDI** (AP Human Resource Development Institute), the State's apex training institute for officials: at **Bapatla** (the Dr MCR HRD Institute stayed in Hyderabad).
[5:0:11] - **Sand policy (September 2019):** **APMDC** mines sand and sells it at government **stockyards** (about ₹370 a tonne) from 5 Sept 2019, ending free sand.
[5:0:12] - **Backward districts:** under the **AP Reorganisation Act, 2014 (Sec. 46, 94)** the Centre gives a special development package to the backward districts of **Rayalaseema and north coastal AP**; the Centre's decision to continue it concerns **Andhra Pradesh**.
[5:0:13] > Retirement age 62 (not 65 or 60); APSRTC Act = 'Absorption of Employees into Government Service'; APHRDI = Bapatla.
[5:0:14] ~~ Sources: CAG Revenue Audit Report 4 of 2014 ch. 11 (Sec. 29(3)); AP Reorganisation Act 2014 p.10, p.24, p.64; Wikipedia 'Emblem of Andhra Pradesh'; indiacode.nic.in (Act 36 of 2019); siasat.com, thenewsminute.com (retirement age); downtoearth.org.in (sand policy); deccanchronicle.com (APHRDI)
## SUB 1 | Naidu-era Framework (2014-19) - Missions, Monitoring and Campaigns
[5:1:0] # Mission-based development (2014, Vision 2029)
[5:1:1] - **7 sectoral missions** (Sept 2014): primary sector, social empowerment, knowledge & skill development, urban development, industry, infrastructure, services.
[5:1:2] - Plus **5 grids** (water, road, power, gas, fibre optic) and **5 campaigns** (e.g. Pedarikampai Gelupu – victory over poverty, **Polam Pilustondi**, Swachh Andhra).
[5:1:3] # Outcome monitoring (from 2016-17)
[5:1:4] TABLE head: Framework | Level | Themes
  [5:1:4.0] Kutumba Vikasam | family | 15
  [5:1:4.1] Samaja Vikasam | community | 10
[5:1:5] - Five monitoring themes in all: GVA, SDGs, Samaja Vikasam, Kutumba Vikasam, KPIs.
[5:1:6] # Janmabhoomi – Maa Vooru
[5:1:7] - Original **Janmabhoomi**: 1 January 1997 (merging Prajala Vaddaku Paalana and Sramadaanam).
[5:1:8] - **3rd round:** **2–11 January 2016**, theme '**Smart Village – Smart Ward** towards Smart Andhra Pradesh' (earlier rounds Oct 2014, Jun 2015).
[5:1:9] # Other Naidu-era initiatives
[5:1:10] - **Smart Water Distribution Monitoring** web portal (SCADA, Vijayawada Municipal Corporation): launched by CM N. Chandrababu Naidu in **November 2016** → **Andhra Pradesh**.
[5:1:11] - **ODF:** **West Godavari** declared open-defecation-free in the last week of **April 2017** (APPSC key; Nallajerla mandal ODF in Oct 2016).
[5:1:12] - **Data-driven governance:** AP keyed as the **first State to apply algorithms and data science in public policy** (RTGS, e-Pragati).
[5:1:13] > Missions 7, grids 5, campaigns 5; Kutumba 15 vs Samaja 10 – the two numbers are swapped in distractors.
[5:1:14] ~~ Sources: business-standard.com (PTI 26 Sep 2014, 7 missions); education.sakshi.com AP SES 2016-17 monitoring chapter (15/10 themes); thehansindia.com 24 Dec 2015 and business-standard.com 2 Jan 2016 (Janmabhoomi round 3); NextGen AP Economy STATIC-1 Part 1 p.12 (Janmabhoomi 1997); gktoday.in (Smart Water portal); thehansindia.com 19 Oct 2016 (WG ODF target)

## SUB 3 | Education Schemes
[5:3:0] TABLE head: Scheme (old name) | Objective / latest figure
  [5:3:0.0] Mana Badi Mana Bhavishyathu (formerly Mana Badi Nadu-Nedu) | school infrastructure upgrade (₹2 lakh per classroom); ₹1,500 crore (2026-27); ₹7,876 crore of Nadu-Nedu works left incomplete; Nadu-Nedu also upgraded PHCs/CHCs
  [5:3:0.1] Sarvepalli Radhakrishnan Vidyarthi Mitra (formerly Jagananna Vidya Kanuka) | free student kits: textbooks, uniforms, shoes, bags, stationery; ₹953.71 crore (Classes I-X) + ₹34.40 crore (XI-XII) for 2025-26
  [5:3:0.2] Akshara Andhra | Adult Education Department's 100% literacy drive (APPSC 2025 key); 24.7 lakh adults passed FLNAT-2026 (34,406 centres, 15 Mar 2026); literacy 73% → 83% (2 Apr 2026)
  [5:3:0.3] Fee reimbursement (Post-Matric) | ₹1,543.17 crore for 5.40 lakh SC students (11 Jun 2026)
  [5:3:0.4] Vidya Shakti | IIT Madras collaboration for STEM coaching, Classes 3-12 (2024-25)
  [5:3:0.5] Talliki Vandanam (formerly Amma Vodi) | education support to mothers
[5:3:1] > Akshara Andhra = 100% literacy programme (APPSC 2025).
[5:3:2] - **Akshara Andhra:** **G.O.Ms.No.37 (25 Nov 2025)**; Adult Education Department; aligned with the Centre's **ULLAS**; target **100% adult literacy** (not Swarna/Sakshara/Chaduvula Andhra).
[5:3:3] - **Other States:** the '**Conquer Exam, Be a Warrior**' exam-stress app = **Gujarat** (IIPH Gandhinagar with the State Health Department, 18 March 2019).
[5:3:4] ~~ Sources: thehansindia.com and apteachers.in (Akshara Andhra, GO 37); Rocket CA 2026 Part 02 p.20-22 (ULLAS); phfi.org press note (Conquer Exam app); app notes 6:5 (sub 3)
## SUB 4 | Platforms, Portals and Police Bodies
[5:4:0] # Apps and portals → purpose
[5:4:1] TABLE head: Platform | Purpose
  [5:4:1.0] RTGS (Real-Time Governance Society) | real-time monitoring hub; added satellite weather alerts for fishermen (April 2026)
  [5:4:1.1] AWARE | predictive governance
  [5:4:1.2] Mana Mitra (WhatsApp Governance, 95523 00009, since Jan 2025) | WhatsApp-based citizen services: temple services, darshan tickets, hall tickets; complaints by voice note (Jun 2026)
  [5:4:1.3] Meekosam (2015) | public grievance portal ("For You"); analytics added Jan 2026
  [5:4:1.4] e-Pragati | unified service portal; 45 more services (Feb 2026)
  [5:4:1.5] e-Seva | launched 2001; later MeeSeva
[5:4:2] - SROs (sub-registrar offices) **not privatised** (Jul 2026).
[5:4:3] # EAGLE and police operations
[5:4:4] - **EAGLE = Elite Anti-Narcotics Group for Law Enforcement**: AP Police **anti-drug force**; **HQ Vijayawada**; head **IGP Ake Ravi Krishna**.
[5:4:5] - **MoU with Indian Red Cross Society** on **28 Aug 2026** to check ganja/drugs at **village level**.
[5:4:6] - Drive: **"EAGLE on Train"** against ganja.
[5:4:7] TABLE head: Operation | Purpose
  [5:4:7.0] Operation Muskaan | child labour
  [5:4:7.1] Red sanders drive | thermal drones against smuggling (Feb 2026)
[5:4:8] - **Prana Raksha** web application (Chittoor Police): to **save the lives of road-accident victims** and **reach women in distress** (APPSC Group-1 key).
[5:4:9] ~~ Sources: printed APPSC key (Prana Raksha, not verified); app notes 6:5 (sub 5)
## SUB 5 | NTR Bharosa Social Security Pensions
[5:5:0] # Category → monthly pension
[5:5:1] - **NTR Bharosa** (renamed from **YSR Pension Kanuka**) is run through **SERP**.
[5:5:2] TABLE head: Category | Monthly pension
  [5:5:2.0] Old age (60+), widows (18+), weavers, toddy tappers, fishermen, single women, transgender persons, artists, others | ₹4,000 (raised from ₹3,000, April 2024)
  [5:5:2.1] Disabled (40%+ disability), leprosy | ₹6,000
  [5:5:2.2] Dialysis / CKDU patients | ₹10,000
  [5:5:2.3] Fully bedridden (bilateral elephantiasis, paralysis) | ₹15,000 (from ₹5,000)
[5:5:3] - **New in 2026**: **spouse pensions** for surviving wives; pensions for **dialysis and organ-transplant** patients (added, not abolished).
[5:5:4] - Delivery: **at the doorstep on the 1st of every month** (**'Pedala Sevalo'** day).
[5:5:5] # Outlay data
[5:5:6] TABLE head: Period | Outlay
  [5:5:6.0] May 2025 | ₹2,758 crore a month
  [5:5:6.1] June 2026 | ₹2,711.79 crore for 62.19 lakh
  [5:5:6.2] 1 September 2026 | ₹2,693 crore to 61.92 lakh beneficiaries
  [5:5:6.3] Cumulative since 2024 (as of 31 Aug 2026) | ₹73,506 crore
  [5:5:6.4] Budget 2026-27 (annual) | ₹27,719 crore
[5:5:7] - The APPSC 2025 question on monthly pension spending (May 2025) was **voided** by APPSC (discrepancy in question/answer); there is no official key.
[5:5:8] - **Disability pension**: AP = **India's highest** (up to ₹15,000) vs **Bihar ₹1,100** (Jun 2026).
[5:5:9] - **16th Finance Commission**: AP has the **highest per-capita pension spending** among States.
[5:5:10] - **2025 monthly outlay:** about **₹2,750 crore** (₹2,750.96 crore released for 1 August 2025); about **₹33,000 crore a year**.
[5:5:11] ~~ Sources: deccanchronicle.com (Aug 2025 release; ₹33,000 crore a year); app notes 6:5 (sub 1)
## SUB 6 | Health Schemes
[5:6:0] # Dr NTR Vaidya Seva
[5:6:1] - Renamed from **(Rajiv/YSR) Aarogyasri**; run by the **Dr NTR Vaidya Seva Trust**.
[5:6:2] - **Cashless care** mainly for **BPL** families: **1.43 crore families** (1.43 crore health cards, APSES 2024-25); **3,257 procedures** (2024-25).
[5:6:3] - **5 more cardiac procedures** added (Mar 2026).
[5:6:4] - Opposition (Mar 2026) called the **₹2.5 lakh insurance plan** a **threat to Aarogyasri**.
[5:6:5] # Other schemes under the Trust
[5:6:6] TABLE head: Scheme | For
  [5:6:6.0] Employees Health Scheme | government employees
  [5:6:6.1] Working Journalists Health Scheme | journalists
  [5:6:6.2] Aarogya Raksha | APL families
  [5:6:6.3] Amrutha | orphans (not marine fishermen)
[5:6:7] # SHRESTH
[5:6:8] - **SHRESTH = State Health Regulatory Excellence Index** (**Aug 2025**): strengthens the health regulatory system by **benchmarking State drug regulators**; **MoHFW**, run by **CDSCO**.
[5:6:9] - **SHRESTH** was launched on **12 August 2025** by the Union Health Ministry; a **national** index (States in manufacturing and distribution categories, monthly data to CDSCO), aligned with WHO GBT Maturity Level 3.
[5:6:10] ~~ Sources: AP Socio-Economic Survey 2024-25 p.225-227 (Trust schemes); biospectrumindia.com 12 Aug 2025 (SHRESTH); app notes 6:5 (sub 2)
## SUB 7 | Administrative Reforms, Conferences, Task Forces and Debates
[5:7:0] # Swarna Gramam & Ward Secretariat
[5:7:1] - The village & ward secretariat system was **renamed "Swarna Gramam & Ward Secretariat"** system.
[5:7:2] - The **2019 secretariat model** and the new **Swarna Gramam** system are the **delivery arms of Super Six welfare**.
[5:7:3] - **1.68 crore households** mapped; **e-KYC for 1.71 crore families** (11 Jun 2026).
[5:7:4] # 8th District Collectors' Conference
[5:7:5] - **Secretariat, 10–11 September 2026**; **19 subjects**, including district roadmaps under Swarna Andhra and Super Six; reviewed **Super Six, P4 and Swarna Andhra @2047**.
[5:7:6] - **Vision 2047 three-tier monitoring**: **District Vision Units**, **Mandal Vision Units**, **Constituency Development Committees**.
[5:7:7] # Task forces and local bodies
[5:7:8] TABLE head: Task force (2026 Cabinet) | Date
  [5:7:8.0] Social-media abuse | July 2026
  [5:7:8.1] Godavari pollution | May 2026
[5:7:9] - Land disputes → **fast-track special courts**.
[5:7:10] - **Zilla Praja Parishads 13 → 28**; ZP and MPTC elections due (8 Sep 2026).
[5:7:11] # 8.3A Sanitation and clean-village campaigns
[5:7:12] TABLE head: Campaign | Launch occasion / coverage
  [5:7:12.0] CLAP = Clean Andhra Pradesh (Jagananna Swachha Sankalpam) | launched on Gandhi Jayanti, 2 October 2021 at Benz Circle, Vijayawada; about 4,000 garbage-collection vehicles flagged off for urban and rural local bodies (door-to-door dry and wet waste)
  [5:7:12.1] Swarna Andhra - Swachh Andhra (Swachh Andhra - Swachh Diwas) | launched by CM N. Chandrababu Naidu in January 2025 (first event at Mydukur, Kadapa district); held on the third Saturday of every month from 18 January 2025 (MA&UD order of 17 Jan 2025); 12 monthly themes (Feb 2025: "Source-Resource"); run with the Swachha Andhra Corporation; June 2026 drive capped a 30-day "Operation Clean Sweep" in urban local bodies
[5:7:13] > CLAP was planned for 8 July 2021 (YSR's birth anniversary) but launched on 2 October 2021 (Gandhi Jayanti).
[5:7:14] # Debates
[5:7:15] - **"New welfarism"** critique (30 Jun 2026): **direct cash transfers** may **crowd out public goods** and **strain State finances**.
[5:7:16] # Conferences
[5:7:17] - **CXO Round Table Conclave** (IT and electronics, post-COVID recovery): **Vijayawada**, **2 April 2021**, IT Minister Mekapati Goutham Reddy.
[5:7:18] ~~ Sources: newkerala.com (ANI, 2 Apr 2021, CXO conclave); app notes 6:5 (sub 7)

## SUB 12 | Number Game and Traps
[5:12:0] # Number game
[5:12:1] TABLE head: Item | Correct Fact
  [5:12:1.0] Standard pension | ₹4,000 (from ₹3,000, Apr 2024)
  [5:12:1.1] Disabled / dialysis / bedridden | ₹6,000 / ₹10,000 / ₹15,000
  [5:12:1.2] Pension outlay May 2025 | ₹2,758 crore a month
  [5:12:1.3] Pension outlay Sep 2026 | ₹2,693 crore to 61.92 lakh
  [5:12:1.4] Pension budget 2026-27 | ₹27,719 crore
  [5:12:1.5] NTR Vaidya Seva | 1.43 crore families; 3,257 procedures
  [5:12:1.6] Housing target | 5.52 lakh houses by March 2027
  [5:12:1.7] ZPPs | 13 → 28
  [5:12:1.8] Public Employment Order-2025 | 6 zones, 2 multi-zones
  [5:12:1.9] Monthly Economic Reports | 651 KPIs
  [5:12:1.10] Local jobs law (July 2019) | 75% private jobs
  [5:12:1.11] Jeeva Kranthi units | 2,22,754
[5:12:2] # FIRST / LAST / SPECIAL FACTS
[5:12:3] - First woman CJ of AP High Court: Justice Lisa Gill.
[5:12:4] - First State to notify a Pedestrian Safety Policy: AP (6 Aug 2026).
[5:12:5] - First overseas investment centre: APEX Korea, Busan.
[5:12:6] - Highest per-capita pension spending among States: AP (16th Finance Commission).
[5:12:7] - Pensions are paid on the 1st of every month (Pedala Sevalo).
[5:12:8] # Traps
[5:12:9] - 1. **SHRESTH** is run by **CDSCO** (MoHFW) and benchmarks State drug regulators.
[5:12:10] - 2. **Amrutha** is for **orphans**, not marine fishermen; **Aarogya Raksha** is for APL families.
[5:12:11] - 3. **RTGS** = monitoring; **AWARE** = predictive; **Meekosam** = grievances; **Mana Mitra** = WhatsApp.
[5:12:12] - 4. **₹2,758 crore** (May 2025) vs **₹2,693 crore** (Sep 2026) vs **₹27,719 crore** (annual budget 2026-27).
[5:12:13] - 5. 2026 **added** pensions for dialysis and organ-transplant patients; nothing was abolished.
[5:12:14] - 6. **Lisa Gill** is HC Chief Justice; **T. Mallikarjuna Rao** is Lokayukta.
[5:12:15] - 7. **Jeeva Kranthi** belongs to **Animal Husbandry**, for YSR Cheyutha women.
[5:12:16] - 8. **Talliki Vandanam** and **NTR Vaidya Seva** are renamed schemes, not new ones.

## SUB 2 | Census 2027 – Digital Census and Caste Count
[30:2:0] # Key facts
[30:2:1] TABLE head: Particular | Fact
  [30:2:1.0] Started | 1 April 2026 (house listing)
  [30:2:1.1] Nature | India's first fully digital census
  [30:2:1.2] Number | 16th overall; 8th after Independence
  [30:2:1.3] Gap | 16 years (not 12) since 2011
  [30:2:1.4] Budget | ₹11,718.24 crore
  [30:2:1.5] Self-enumeration | Online portal in 16 languages
[30:2:2] - **2021 Census** postponed due to **COVID-19**.
[30:2:3] # Phases and reference dates
[30:2:4] TABLE head: Item | Detail
  [30:2:4.0] Phase I | April–September 2026 – house listing
  [30:2:4.1] Phase II | February 2027 – population, socio-economic data, caste
  [30:2:4.2] Reference date, most of India | 1 March 2027
  [30:2:4.3] Reference date, snow-bound areas | 1 October 2026 – Ladakh, J&K, Himachal Pradesh, Uttarakhand
[30:2:5] # Caste and legal basis
[30:2:6] - **First caste count since 1931** – caste of every individual (earlier only SCs and STs); legal basis **Section 3 of the Census Act, 1948**.
[30:2:7] TABLE head: Item | Fact
  [30:2:7.0] Act / Rules | Census Act, 1948; Census Rules, 1990
  [30:2:7.1] Conducted by | Registrar General and Census Commissioner of India (MHA)
  [30:2:7.2] Subject list | Union List Entry 69
  [30:2:7.3] Seats link | Articles 81 and 82 – Lok Sabha seats and delimitation
[30:2:8] # Census milestones

  [30:2:9.0] 1872 | First census
  [30:2:9.1] 1881 | First complete census
  [30:2:9.2] 1951 | First census after Independence
  [30:2:9.3] 2011 | Last census – 121.08 crore

## SUB 3 | Judgments, Ordinances and the Judiciary
[30:3:0] # Presidential Reference on Governors' assent (20 Nov 2025)
[30:3:1] - **Bench:** five-judge Constitution Bench led by **CJI B.R. Gavai**, on a Reference under **Article 143**.
[30:3:2] - **Held:** courts **cannot fix timelines** for the Governor (Art 200) or President (Art 201); **no "deemed assent"**.
[30:3:3] - **Nature:** an Art 143 opinion is **advisory** – not "law declared" under **Article 141**.
[30:3:4] # Supreme Court judges – Ordinance to Act
[30:3:5] TABLE head: Step | Fact
  [30:3:5.0] Ordinance | Promulgated 16 May 2026 under Article 123
  [30:3:5.1] Change | 33 → 37 judges excluding the CJI (34 → 38 with the CJI)
  [30:3:5.2] Act | Assent 11 August 2026 (Act No. 14 of 2026)
[30:3:6] # Judicial infrastructure
[30:3:7] - **eCourts Phase III:** Cabinet **13 Sep 2023**; Central Sector Scheme; **₹7,210 crore**; **1,150 virtual courts**; **4,400 eSewa Kendras**.
[30:3:8] - **Fast Track Special Courts (rape, POCSO):** **Centrally Sponsored Scheme** (2023–26), ₹1,952.23 crore; Central share from the **Nirbhaya Fund**.
[30:3:9] # Legal services (NALSA)
[30:3:10] - **Justice Vikram Nath** – Executive Chairman of NALSA from **24 Nov 2025**; **Justice J.K. Maheshwari** – Chairman, SC Legal Services Committee.
[30:3:11] - **NALSA Veer Parivar Sahayata Yojana, 2025:** legal aid for **families of soldiers**; launched at **Srinagar**.

[30:4:3] # Renaming – Kerala to Keralam
[30:4:4] - **Kerala (Alteration of Name) Bill, 2026:** passed August 2026 under **Article 3**; amends the **First Schedule**; an ordinary law, **not an Article 368 amendment**.
[30:4:5] # Acts and Bills
[30:4:6] - **IBC (Amendment) Bill, 2026:** brings a **creditor-initiated insolvency resolution process**.
[30:4:7] - **Bills of Lading Act, 2025** repealed the Act of **1856**; **Merchant Shipping Act, 2025** replaced the Act of **1958**.
[30:4:8] - **Constitution (130th Amendment) Bill, 2025:** removal after **30 consecutive days** in custody; amends **Arts 75, 164 and 239AA**.
[30:4:9] - **EWS quota:** **103rd Amendment, 2019** – up to 10%; upheld in **Janhit Abhiyan v UoI** (Nov 2022, 3:2).
[30:4:10] # Parliament – Monsoon Session 2026
[30:4:11] - **20 July – 13 August 2026**; 12 Bills introduced, **11 passed by both Houses**; pending: **Indian Statistical Institute Bill, 2026**.

=== NOTES 6:51 | Overview and data: National - committees, commissions and their reports
## SUB 0 | Committees – Chairperson and Subject (PYQ)
[51:0:0] # PYQ committees
[51:0:1] TABLE head: Committee | Subject
  [51:0:1.0] Indrajit Gupta (1998; report 1999) | State funding of elections – electoral reforms (not Centre–State relations, not banking)
  [51:0:1.1] Vijay Kelkar (2015) | Revisiting and revitalising the PPP model of infrastructure
  [51:0:1.2] N.K. Singh (2016) | FRBM Act review
  [51:0:1.3] Kumar Mangalam Birla (1999, SEBI) | Corporate governance
  [51:0:1.4] J.J. Irani (2004) | Company Law
[51:0:2] - Indrajit Gupta (former Home Minister) headed an **eight-member** committee; it held State funding **constitutionally and legally justified** – partial, **in kind**, for recognised parties.
[51:0:3] - **Dinanath Batra** (Shiksha Sanskriti Utthan Nyas, 2015): an **education commission "just like the Election Commission"**, insulated from regime change, run by a **Bharatiya Education Service**; also a 31-member Non-Governmental Education Commission (2014). Not D.S. Kothari (Education Commission 1964–66) or Acharya Ramamurti (NPE review, 1990).
[51:0:4] > Exam angle: match-the-following (Kelkar–PPP, N.K. Singh–FRBM, Birla–corporate governance, Irani–company law) and "Indrajit Gupta = electoral reforms" are the repeat APPSC angles.
[51:0:5] # Other committees – chair and subject
[51:0:6] TABLE head: Chair | Committee and subject
  [51:0:6.0] Rajiv Kumar (2019) | Panel to sell 149 State-owned oil and gas fields (asset sale; PYQ)
  [51:0:6.1] K. Kasturirangan | Draft NEP 2019 → NEP 2020 (asked 2018, 2019)
  [51:0:6.2] Dinesh Goswami (1990) | Electoral reforms
  [51:0:6.3] Indrajit Gupta (1998) | State funding of elections (partial, in kind, for recognised parties)
  [51:0:6.4] Urjit Patel (2014) | Inflation targeting 4% ±2%; accepted → 2016 RBI Act amendment
  [51:0:6.5] Nachiket Mor | Financial inclusion
  [51:0:6.6] Shanta Kumar | FCI–PDS reform
  [51:0:6.7] Ashok Dalwai | Doubling farmers' income
  [51:0:6.8] M.N. Venkatachaliah (2002) | Review of the working of the Constitution
  [51:0:6.9] Santhanam (1962-63) | Prevention of corruption → led to the CBI
  [51:0:6.10] Yashpal (2009) | NCHER proposal
  [51:0:6.11] Ram Nath Kovind (High-Level Committee) | Simultaneous elections
[51:0:7] - **Vijay Kelkar Committee (2015):** revisiting and revitalising the **PPP model** of infrastructure; report to the Finance Minister **November 2015**.
[51:0:8] - **Uday Kotak Committee (2017):** set up by **SEBI** on **corporate governance** of listed companies; report **October 2017**.
[51:0:9] - **Sanjay Agrawal Committee (July 2022):** former Agriculture Secretary; on **MSP**, **natural farming** and **crop diversification**.
[51:0:10] - **M. Narasimham Committees (banking reforms):** **Committee on the Financial System (1991)** and **Committee on Banking Sector Reforms (1998)**, both chaired by **M. Narasimham**; recommended lower SLR/CRR, entry of private banks and stronger capital adequacy norms.
[51:0:11] # Kovind Committee on Simultaneous Elections
[51:0:12] - Set up **2 September 2023**; report to the President on **14 March 2024** (**18,626 pages**).
[51:0:13] - Proposed **Article 82A** (synchronise terms of Lok Sabha and State Assemblies) and **Article 324A** (municipal and panchayat polls with general elections).
[51:0:14] - Lok Sabha and Assembly polls first; local body polls to follow; amendment for Art. 82A needs **no State ratification**.
[51:0:15] - **Constitution (129th Amendment) Bill, 2024** introduced in the Lok Sabha in **December 2024** and sent to a **JPC**.

[51:1:4] - First head **Justice K. Venkataswami**; resigned **November 2002** (accepted 24 Nov 2002).
[51:1:5] - Replaced by **Justice S.N. Phukan** (retired SC judge), notified January 2003; commission wound up **2004**.
[51:1:6] # PM's Economic Advisory Council
[51:1:7] - **2009–2014 (UPA-II): five members** – Chairman **C. Rangarajan** + Suman K. Bery, Saumitra Chaudhuri, M. Govinda Rao, V.S. Vyas; members had Minister of State rank.
[51:1:8] - Ended 2014; **EAC-PM reconstituted 25 September 2017**; chair now **S. Mahendra Dev** (2025).

## SUB 3 | Law Commission of India
[51:3:0] # History and Nature
[51:3:1] - Colonial precursor: **Macaulay's** Law Commission produced the **IPC (1860)**.
[51:3:2] - First post-independence Law Commission: **1955**, chaired by **M.C. Setalvad**.
[51:3:3] - Nature: **neither constitutional nor statutory**; an **executive, advisory** body set up by a Government of India order for a **fixed 3-year term**.
[51:3:4] - Reports to the **Ministry of Law and Justice**, not the President.
[51:3:5] - **23rd Law Commission**: constituted **September 2024** for 3 years; Chairperson **Justice Dinesh Maheshwari** (former SC judge), took charge **15 April 2025**; term up to **31 August 2027**.
[51:3:6] - **21st Law Commission**: 2018 Consultation Paper on Family Law → **UCC "neither necessary nor desirable at this stage"** (18 Sep 2026).
[51:3:7] # Law Commission Reports – Number and Subject
[51:3:8] TABLE head: Report | Subject
  [51:3:8.0] 14th (1958) | Reform of judicial administration
  [51:3:8.1] 59th (1974) | Family courts → Family Courts Act, 1984
  [51:3:8.2] 114th (1986) | Gram Nyayalayas → Gram Nyayalayas Act, 2008
  [51:3:8.3] 121st (1987) | National Judicial Service Commission
  [51:3:8.4] 170th (1999) | Electoral reform: raise Lok Sabha strength by 25% through a party list
  [51:3:8.5] 214th (2008) | Review of the procedure for appointing judges
  [51:3:8.6] 230th | "Uncle judge" syndrome in High Courts
  [51:3:8.7] 245th | Arrears and pendency
  [51:3:8.8] 255th (2015) | Electoral reforms: EVM totalisers; de-register parties that skip elections for 10 years
  [51:3:8.9] 260th | Draft model Bilateral Investment Treaty
  [51:3:8.10] 279th (2023) | Sedition – retain with safeguards
[51:3:9] # Accepted or Pending
[51:3:10] - **Accepted and legislated:** 59th (Family Courts Act 1984) and 114th (Gram Nyayalayas Act 2008).
[51:3:11] - **Pending:** 255th report on **totalisers** (SC sought the Centre's view, **2 Sep 2026**); **All India Judicial Service** still pending.
[51:3:12] > 59th = family courts; 114th = Gram Nyayalayas; 279th = sedition; 255th = EVM totalisers.
[51:3:13] ~~ Sources: app notes 6:51 (sub 1)
## SUB 4 | New Panels and Expert Groups 2025-26
[51:4:0] # Exams and NTA
[51:4:1] - **Nandan Nilekani task force**: leak-proof, **technology-driven exams**; set up after the **NEET-UG 2026 leak** (27 Jul 2026).
[51:4:2] - Earlier: **K. Radhakrishnan Committee** on **NTA reform**.
[51:4:3] - **NTA** is only a **society** under the **Societies Registration Act, 1860**; after the 2026 NEET leak, an SC petition sought **statutory status** (17 May 2026).
[51:4:4] - **Justice R. Subhash Reddy** panel: police action in NEET protests.
[51:4:5] - **Pankaj Kumar Singh** committee: **Gorkha issue** (MHA, 23 Aug 2026).

## SUB 6 | Higher-Education Regulator and Parliamentary Committee Reports
[51:6:0] - **NCHER** (National Commission for Higher Education and Research): proposed after the **Yashpal Committee (2009)**; announced by HRD Minister **Kapil Sibal**; **never enacted**.
[51:6:1] - **Viksit Bharat Shiksha Adhishthan (VBSA) Bill, 2025** (Ministry of Education): **single apex body** to replace **UGC, AICTE, NCTE** and other regulators (10 Jul 2026).
[51:6:2] - Sent to a **Joint Parliamentary Committee (JPC)**, which flagged **centralisation**.
[51:6:3] - In the JPC, **Andhra Pradesh, Meghalaya and Madhya Pradesh** objected to centralising provisions (10 Jul 2026).
[51:6:4] - After AP objected, the Centre agreed that **State consent** is needed for decisions on colleges of State universities (18 Jul 2026).
[51:6:5] - **Not passed** in the Monsoon Session 2026 (14 Aug 2026).
[51:6:6] - **Parliamentary Standing Committee on Transport, Tourism and Culture**: **393rd Report "From Manuscript to Mission"** (manuscripts + AI) (13 Aug 2026).
[51:6:7] ~~ Sources: app notes 6:51 (sub 5); app notes 6:51 (sub 6)

## SUB 8 | NCRB Reports – Crime in India 2024, ADSI 2024
[51:8:0] - NCRB: under **MHA**; set up **1986** by executive order after the **Tandon Committee**.
[51:8:1] - **Crime in India 2024:** cybercrime **+17.9%**; conviction rate **53.3%** (May 2026); highest **human-trafficking** cases = **Telangana**; highest **violent-crime rate** = **Odisha**.
[51:8:2] - **ADSI 2024:** about **1.7 lakh suicides**; **31% daily-wage workers**; **7,874 building-collapse deaths** in 2020-24 (8 Sep 2026).
[51:8:3] - **Prison Statistics India**: also NCRB.
[51:8:4] - Criticism: delayed NCRB/AISHE data (31 Aug 2026).
[51:8:5] - **ADSI 2024** released **6 May 2026**: accidental deaths **4,67,857** (33.3 per lakh); road deaths 1,75,142.

=== NOTES 6:57 | Overview and data: International - summits, conferences and their outcomes
## SUB 0 | World Economic Forum – Davos Meetings and Initiatives
[57:0:0] # The WEF and its venue
[57:0:1] - **WEF:** non-profit foundation founded **1971 by Klaus Schwab**; based at **Cologny (Geneva)**; **Annual Meeting** every January at **Davos(-Klosters), Switzerland**.
[57:0:2] - **"Summer Davos"** (Annual Meeting of the New Champions) is held in **China** – June 2026 at **Dalian**.
[57:0:3] TABLE head: Annual Meeting | Venue / date | Theme or focus
  [57:0:3.0] 2016 | Davos, Switzerland, January | Mastering the Fourth Industrial Revolution
  [57:0:3.1] 2017 | Davos | Responsive and Responsible Leadership (Xi Jinping attended)
  [57:0:3.2] 2023 | Davos, 16–20 January | Cooperation in a Fragmented World (IMF's Gita Gopinath only a panellist, not chair)
  [57:0:3.3] 2025 | Davos | AI governance and climate change ("Collaboration for the Intelligent Age")
  [57:0:3.4] 2026 | Davos, January | Canada PM Carney: "rupture in the world order", call for middle powers; Davos Compact on AMR launched
[57:0:4] > "Where was the WEF meeting held?" – always **Switzerland (Davos)**, whatever the year; Summer Davos is the China trap.
[57:0:5] # WEF initiatives asked
[57:0:6] - **The Great Reset** (June 2020): WEF's initiative for **post-COVID recovery of the global economy** – not World Bank, OECD or UNCTAD.
[57:0:7] - **100 Million Farmers**: WEF multistakeholder platform for **net-zero, nature-positive, resilient food and water systems** by 2030 (UPSC 2024).
[57:0:8] - **Board of Peace** (WEF Davos, Jan 2026): **27 members**; launched by the **US President** for **Gaza reconstruction**; **India only an observer (no vote)**.
[57:0:9] ~~ Sources: Praveen CA-360 Feb 2026 (src/current-affairs/praveen-ca360-2026-02-magazine.txt) p.84 (Davos, Switzerland); web: etradeforall.org "Davos 2023 – Cooperation in a Fragmented World"; app notes 6:57 sub 5 (Carpediem CA)

## SUB 4 | G20 – Structure, Summits, India 2023, Johannesburg 2025
[57:4:0] # Structure
[57:4:1] - Established **1999**; first Leaders' Summit **2008**; members: **19 countries + EU + African Union**.
[57:4:2] - Works through **Finance track + Sherpa track**.
[57:4:3] - Presidency rotates annually among **five regional groups**; **Troika = past + present + next** presidencies.
[57:4:4] # Summits by edition
[57:4:5] TABLE head: Edition | Year | Venue
  [57:4:5.0] 12th | July 2017 | Hamburg, Germany
  [57:4:5.1] 13th | 2018 | Buenos Aires, Argentina
  [57:4:5.2] 14th | 2019 | Osaka, Japan
  [57:4:5.3] 15th | 2020 | Riyadh (virtual)
  [57:4:5.4] 17th | 2022 | Bali – "Recover Together, Recover Stronger"
  [57:4:5.5] 18th | 2023 | New Delhi
  [57:4:5.6] 20th | November 2025 | Johannesburg
[57:4:6] - **G20 Digital Economy Ministerial Meeting**: **Salta, Argentina** (August 2018); Bengaluru (August 2023).
[57:4:7] # India's 2023 Presidency (18th G20)
[57:4:8] TABLE head: Particular | Fact
  [57:4:8.0] Summit | 18th G20 Summit, New Delhi (Delhi), 9–10 September 2023, Bharat Mandapam
  [57:4:8.1] Theme | "One Earth, One Family, One Future" (Vasudhaiva Kutumbakam)
  [57:4:8.2] Presidency term | 1 December 2022 – 30 November 2023
  [57:4:8.3] Meetings | about 60 cities in all States/UTs
  [57:4:8.4] Headline outcome | African Union admitted as permanent member
[57:4:9] - Launched under India's Presidency: **Global Biofuels Alliance (GBA)** (9 Sep 2023); **MAHARISHI** (millets research initiative); Global Initiative on Digital Health; "G20 satellite" for climate monitoring.
[57:4:10] # 20th G20, Johannesburg (22–23 November 2025)
[57:4:11] - Host **South Africa**; **first G20 Summit on African soil**.

[63:1:6] - **League of Nations (1920):** the **USA never joined** – the Senate did not ratify the Treaty of Versailles; China, British India, Cuba were members.
[63:1:7] - **BIMSTEC chair 2026:** **Bangladesh**.
[63:1:8] - **International Solar Alliance:** **Malta** became its **119th member**.
[63:1:9] - **APEC 2025 summit:** **Gyeongju, South Korea**; China proposed **WAICO** for AI governance.
[63:1:10] - **Maastricht criteria** (Eurozone entry): fiscal deficit **below 3% of GDP**; debt **below 60%**; membership of **ERM II**.

  [63:8:1.1] ECTA | Australia | -

  [63:8:1.3] CETA | UK | "Gold standard" deal
  [63:8:1.4] CEPA | Oman | In force Jun 2026; 99% of India's exports duty-free; India's second GCC CEPA
  [63:8:1.5] FTA | EU | "Mother of All Deals"; with an Investment Protection Agreement and a GI Agreement
[63:8:2] - India's FTAs (**June 2026**): **15 FTAs covering 27 countries**.
[63:8:3] # GATT to WTO
[63:8:4] TABLE head: Term | Fact
  [63:8:4.0] GATT | Signed 1947; in force 1948
  [63:8:4.1] Uruguay Round | 8th GATT round, from 1986; concluded 15 Dec 1993; Marrakesh Agreement 15 Apr 1994; WTO from 1 Jan 1995
  [63:8:4.2] Dunkel Draft (1991) | Proposals of GATT DG Arthur Dunkel during the Uruguay Round
  [63:8:4.3] TRIMs | Trade-Related Investment Measures
[63:8:5] # Subsidy boxes and Peace Clause
[63:8:6] TABLE head: Box | Meaning | Limit
  [63:8:6.0] Amber | Trade-distorting domestic support | Capped (de minimis limits)
  [63:8:6.1] Blue | Support tied to production-limiting programmes | -
  [63:8:6.2] Green | Minimal or no trade distortion | Not capped
[63:8:7] - **Peace Clause (Bali, 2013):** shields developing countries' **public food-stockholding** from WTO disputes.
[63:8:8] - **MC14:** **Yaoundé, Cameroon, Mar 2026**; deadlock; **e-commerce moratorium lapsed**.
[63:8:9] - Criticisms: Appellate Body paralysed; voting imbalance in Bretton Woods institutions (quota-based voting not abolished); MC14 failure + U.S. exits = **weakening multilateralism**.
[63:8:10] ~~ Sources: Rocket CA 2026 Part 02 p.80 (WTO); app notes 6:63 sub 7
## SUB 9 | IMF, World Bank Group and Multilateral Banks
[63:9:0] # IMF
[63:9:1] - **France** = **first borrower** from the IMF; lends mainly for **balance-of-payments** problems.
[63:9:2] - **Rapid Financing Instrument:** quick liquidity **without a full programme** (disasters, pandemics, post-conflict).
[63:9:3] - **Structural Adjustment Programmes (1980s-90s):** austerity, privatisation, devaluation.
[63:9:4] - **SDR** = "**paper gold**"; basket of **five currencies** – US dollar, euro, **Chinese yuan**, yen, pound.
[63:9:5] - India's IMF **voting share about 2.63%** (after the 2010 reforms).
[63:9:6] - **BRICS:** about **20% of world GDP** but 11% of IMF votes in **2011**.
[63:9:7] # World Bank Group and other banks
[63:9:8] TABLE head: Body | Fact
  [63:9:8.0] IFC | Private-sector arm of the World Bank Group
  [63:9:8.1] IDA (1960) | Zero- or low-interest loans and grants to the poorest countries
  [63:9:8.2] New Development Bank | 2014, Fortaleza Declaration; HQ Shanghai; founders' vote cannot fall below 55%; no veto for any country
  [63:9:8.3] AIIB | Est. 2016; 111 approved members; HQ Beijing
  [63:9:8.4] BRICS Contingency Reserve Arrangement | IMF-type liquidity support for BRICS members
[63:9:9] # Multilateral lending → project
[63:9:10] TABLE head: Lender | Project
  [63:9:10.0] World Bank + ADB + HUDCO | Amaravati – about ₹26,000 crore facilitated by the Centre
  [63:9:10.1] World Bank | SALT road and school projects, Andhra Pradesh
  [63:9:10.2] AIIB + World Bank | Mission Schools of Excellence, Gujarat
  [63:9:10.3] NDB with ADB | Delhi-Ghaziabad-Meerut RRTS
  [63:9:10.4] World Bank | Bangladesh's Gazipur-Elenga (N4) and Natore-Nawabganj (N6) highways
[63:9:11] ~~ Sources: Rocket CA 2026 Part 02 p.73-74; app notes 6:63 sub 8

2. World Development Report 2025, 'Standards for Development', is an IMF report.
Which of the above statement(s) is/are correct?
  *1 only | 2 only | Both 1 and 2 | Neither 1 nor 2
  x: Global Economic Prospects is issued in January and June (India's FY26 forecast raised from 6.3% to 7.2%); the World Development Report is a World Bank report.
[n51e1ae380db] sub10
  In January 2026, to what did the IMF raise India's 2025-26 growth projection?
  6.6 per cent | *7.3 per cent | 6.2 per cent | 7.2 per cent
  x: The IMF's January 2026 WEO raised FY26 to 7.3% (from 6.6%); 6.2% was its April 2026 FY27 figure and 7.2% the World Bank's January figure.
[nb0ebfa6586e] sub10
  Which one of the following pairs is correctly matched?
  *Logistics Performance Index — World Bank | Corruption Perceptions Index — World Economic Forum | Global Gender Gap Index — INSEAD | Travel and Tourism Development Index — Transparency International
  x: The Logistics Performance Index is published by the World Bank. The Corruption Perceptions Index is published by Transparency International, while the Global Gender Gap and Travel and Tourism Development indices are associated with the World Economic Forum.
[fda5c8857edc8] sub10
  What was India's rank in the World Bank's Logistics Performance Index 2023?
  44th of 139 | 6th of 139 | 8th of 139 | *38th of 139
  x: India was 38th of 139 countries in the LPI 2023.
[faad041f05cc6] sub10
  Who publishes the World Economic Outlook?
  World Bank | *IMF | UNCTAD | World Economic Forum
  x: WEO is the IMF's report; Global Economic Prospects is the World Bank's and the World Investment Report UNCTAD's.
[fac1c8149c510] sub10
  According to the WTO, what was India's rank as an exporter of commercial services in 2024?
  9th | *8th | 4th | 6th
  x: India was the 8th-largest exporter (4.3%) and 9th-largest importer (3.4%) of commercial services in 2024.
[g4f9a64ccbf86] sub10
  How many members does the OECD, headquartered in Paris, have?
  34 | 36 | *38 | 40
  x: The OECD has its headquarters in Paris and has 38 members.
[g1cea650e8a5c] sub10
  Which organisation publishes the Global Gender Gap Report?
  UN Women | *World Economic Forum | UNDP | Transparency International
  x: The Global Gender Gap and the Travel and Tourism Development reports are published by the World Economic Forum.

## SUB 8 | India's Foreign Policy Doctrines
[69:8:0] # Doctrines – Origin and Essence
[69:8:1] TABLE head: Doctrine | Origin | Essence / feature
  [69:8:1.0] Neighbourhood First | 2014 (SAARC leaders invited to the PM's swearing-in) | Priority to neighbours: aid, infrastructure, connectivity, disaster response
  [69:8:1.1] Act East | 2014; upgraded from Look East (1991) | Strategic ties with ASEAN, Japan, South Korea; meets Thailand's "Act West"
  [69:8:1.2] Link West | – | Gulf / West Asia: energy and diaspora ties
  [69:8:1.3] IPOI (Indo-Pacific Oceans Initiative) | East Asia Summit, Bangkok, 2019 | 7 pillars
  [69:8:1.4] SAGAR | 2015 | Indian Ocean Region (IOR)
  [69:8:1.5] MAHASAGAR | 2025 | Global South
  [69:8:1.6] SHANTI | 2026 | Securing Holistic Advancement through Norms, Trust, and Integrity – cooperative maritime security in the Bay of Bengal
  [69:8:1.7] Nuclear doctrine | 2003 | No First Use, credible minimum deterrence
[69:8:2] - Nuclear doctrine unchanged despite **12 "operationally deployed" warheads** (30 Jun 2026).
[69:8:3] > SAGAR (2015) = IOR; MAHASAGAR (2025) = Global South; SHANTI (2026) = Bay of Bengal.
[69:8:4] > India's Act East meets Thailand's "Act West"; India's own Gulf policy is **Link West**, not Act West.
[69:8:5] # Related Terms
[69:8:6] - **Soft Power** – coined by **Joseph Nye** (late 1980s): shaping others' choices through attraction and persuasion, not force.
[69:8:7] - **Minilateralism** – term credited to **Miles Kahler (1992)**.
[69:8:8] - **Raksha (3 Sep 2026)** – Defence Ministry's **10-year framework for defence diplomacy**.

## SUB 1 | Space Missions, Instruments and Astronomy Firsts
[75:1:0] # Missions and instruments → country / agency
[75:1:1] TABLE head: Mission / instrument | Agency or country | Fact
  [75:1:1.0] Phoenix (2008) | NASA | Mars lander that studied water ice near the Martian north pole
  [75:1:1.1] MOXIE | USA (NASA/MIT) | Made oxygen from Martian CO2 on the Perseverance rover – for human exploration of Mars
  [75:1:1.2] X-Calibur | US scientists (NASA balloon) | Measures polarisation of X-rays from black holes and neutron stars
  [75:1:1.3] Geotail (1992-2022) | NASA-JAXA | Studied Earth's magnetosphere; ended after 30 years (last data recorder failed; announced Jan 2023)
  [75:1:1.4] Shiyan-14, Shiyan-15 | China | Experimental satellites, Kuaizhou-1A, Taiyuan (Sep 2022)
  [75:1:1.5] Zhuque-2 (LandSpace) | China | World's first methane-liquid oxygen rocket to reach orbit (12 Jul 2023)
  [75:1:1.6] GL-10 "Greased Lightning" | NASA | Battery-electric, 10-engine tilt-wing VTOL plane tested in 2015
  [75:1:1.7] OSIRIS-REx | NASA | Returned Bennu samples in 2023
  [75:1:1.8] OSIRIS-APEX | NASA | Renamed OSIRIS-REx; heading for asteroid Apophis
  [75:1:1.9] Proba-3 | ESA | Two-spacecraft formation flying to study the Sun's corona
  [75:1:1.10] NASA Swift Boost | NASA | Swift Boost mission using a LINK space-tug
  [75:1:1.11] Japan's H3 | JAXA | Rocket for Chandrayaan-5/LUPEX
  [75:1:1.12] Falcon 9 | SpaceX | Launched GalaxEye's Mission Drishti
[75:1:2] - **First image of a black hole** (M87*, April 2019, Event Horizon Telescope): imaging algorithm led by **Katie Bouman**.
[75:1:3] - Asteroid in news: **2024 YR4**.
[75:1:4] - **Bennu** = OSIRIS-REx samples (2023); **Apophis** = OSIRIS-APEX target.
[75:1:5] > Country traps: methane rocket, Shiyan satellites, AG600, dark-matter lab = **China**; MOXIE, Phoenix, GL-10 = **USA/NASA**.

  [75:4:3.3] NARL | Gadanki, Chittoor (AP)

## SUB 5 | Vaccines, Drugs, Therapies and Regulators
[75:5:0] # Country firsts in immunisation
[75:5:1] - **Pakistan** (15 Nov 2019): first country to put the WHO-recommended **typhoid conjugate vaccine (TCV)** into routine immunisation.
[75:5:2] - **Cameroon** (22 Jan 2024): first to add a malaria vaccine (**RTS,S**) to **routine child immunisation** (after the Ghana-Kenya-Malawi pilot); **R21/Matrix-M** made by **Serum Institute**.
[75:5:3] TABLE head: Product | Developer | Disease / fact
  [75:5:3.0] NexCAR19 | ImmunoACT (IIT Bombay) + Tata Memorial Centre | India's first indigenous CAR-T cell therapy (targets CD19, B-cell cancers); CDSCO market approval 2023
  [75:5:3.1] Covishield | Oxford-AstraZeneca; made by Serum Institute (India) | COVID-19
  [75:5:3.2] R78C | Oxford + Serum Institute of India | Malaria vaccine; targets blood-stage antigens RIPR and CyPRA
  [75:5:3.3] Coartem Baby | Novartis + Medicines for Malaria Venture | First malaria drug for newborns; WHO-prequalified (May 2026)
  [75:5:3.4] Ervebo (rVSV-ZEBOV) | - | Single-dose Ebola vaccine; DRC received 70,000 doses, but not licensed for the Bundibugyo virus (Aug 2026)
  [75:5:3.5] Nafithromycin (Miqnaf) | Wockhardt | First indigenous macrolide antibiotic; drug-resistant pneumonia
  [75:5:3.6] IndOss | CSIR-NCL | Indigenous bone substitute
  [75:5:3.7] Qdenga | Takeda (Japan) | Tetravalent dengue vaccine
  [75:5:3.8] Wegovy (semaglutide) | Novo Nordisk | New use for fatty liver disease (CDSCO, Jul 2026)
  [75:5:3.9] Nivolumab + relatlimab | Bristol Myers | Melanoma; first LAG-3 dual immunotherapy in India (CDSCO, Jul 2026)
  [75:5:3.10] BIRSA 101 | Indian | India's first CRISPR-based gene therapy; sickle cell disease (tribal focus)
[75:5:4] - **Monoclonal antibodies**: target **specific molecules**; used in **cancer** and **autoimmune** treatment.
[75:5:5] - **Advanced Therapeutic Products** (from **Jul 2026**): gene therapies, cell/CAR-T products, xenografts come under the **Central Licence Approving Authority** (amendment to the **Drugs Rules, 1945**; not DBT).
[75:5:6] TABLE head: Regulator | Role
  [75:5:6.0] CDSCO (headed by DCGI) | Clinical-trial and marketing approval
  [75:5:6.1] DBT | Genetic manipulation and biosafety
  [75:5:6.2] Central Licence Approving Authority | Advanced Therapeutic Products (from Jul 2026)
[75:5:7] - **Polio**: India certified **polio-free in 2014**.
[75:5:8] - **Rhodamine-B:** industrial dye not allowed in food; **Tamil Nadu** banned **cotton candy** over it (2024).

[75:6:0] # Asked in PYQs

[75:6:3] - **AG600 (Kunlong)**: **China's** – world's largest amphibious aircraft (lands on water and land).

  [75:6:5.0] LRLACM | DRDO | Subsonic land-attack cruise missile; range over 1,000 km; Manik engine; replaces Nirbhay
  [75:6:5.1] Astra Mk 1 | DRDO | Beyond-visual-range air-to-air missile; first export to Indonesia
  [75:6:5.2] Agni-4 | DRDO / Strategic Forces Command | Nuclear-capable, two-stage solid-fuel ballistic missile, range about 4,000 km (PMF: MRBM; Praveen CA: IRBM) – not an ICBM

## SUB 7 | Indian and Joint Space Missions 2025-26
[75:7:0] # NISAR – NASA-ISRO Synthetic Aperture Radar
[75:7:1] - **NISAR** = the **first ISRO-NASA joint satellite** and the **world's first dual-frequency radar satellite**; launched **30 Jul 2025** by **GSLV-F16** from **Sriharikota**.
[75:7:2] - Dual radar: **L-band SAR by NASA** + **S-band SAR by ISRO**; cost **$1.5 billion**; scans Earth **every 12 days**.
[75:7:3] - Objectives: **land deformation** (earthquakes, landslides, **land subsidence**), **ice sheets and glaciers**, ecosystems and **biomass**, disasters.
[75:7:4] TABLE head: Mission | Agency / builder | Vehicle | Key fact
  [75:7:4.0] NISAR (30 Jul 2025) | NASA-ISRO | GSLV-F16 | Dual-frequency radar; $1.5 billion; every 12 days
  [75:7:4.1] BlueBird Block-2 (24 Dec 2025) | US firm AST SpaceMobile | LVM3-M6 | 6,100 kg: heaviest payload from Indian soil
  [75:7:4.2] EOS-05 (ex-GISAT-1A) | ISRO | GSLV-F17 | India's first Earth-imaging satellite in geosynchronous orbit
  [75:7:4.3] EOS-N1 | ISRO | PSLV-C62 | Failed
  [75:7:4.4] TRISHNA | ISRO + CNES (France) | Planned on a PSLV | Thermal-infrared satellite
  [75:7:4.5] Chandrayaan-5 / LUPEX | ISRO-JAXA | Japan's H3 rocket (~2028) | Lunar south-pole mission; not on LVM3
  [75:7:4.6] Mission Drishti | GalaxEye | Falcon 9 | World's first OptoSAR satellite
  [75:7:4.7] Vikram-1 (Mission 'Aagaman', 18 Jul 2026) | Skyroot Aerospace, Hyderabad | - | India the third country (after US, China) with a private orbital rocket
[75:7:5] # Gaganyaan, Vyommitra and ISRO
[75:7:6] - **Gaganyaan**: crew of three to a 400 km orbit for three days; ground-tracking support from **ESA** (not NASA); IADT-02 (10 Apr 2026) validated the crew-module parachutes before the uncrewed G1 flight.
[75:7:7] - **Vyommitra**: **semi-humanoid** robot for the **uncrewed G1 and G2** flights.
[75:7:8] - After **two consecutive PSLV failures**, ISRO staff unions questioned **outsourcing under the 2023 Space Policy** (not abolished); about **100 ISRO staff quit**; DoS memo 14 Jul 2026.
[75:7:9] - **Leif Erikson Lunar Prize (2023):** given to **ISRO** for **Chandrayaan-3** by the **Exploration Museum, Husavik, Iceland**.
[75:7:10] - **AP angle:** **NARL** at **Gadanki, Chittoor**.
[75:7:11] ~~ Sources: Rocket CA 2026 Part 01 (src/current-affairs/rocket-ca-2026-01.txt) p.35 (BlueBird Block-2, LVM3-M6), p.57 (LUPEX); Part 02 p.54, p.97 (Vikram-1, Mission Aagaman, 18 Jul 2026); web: businesstoday.in "ISRO receives Leif Erikson Lunar Prize" (21 Dec 2023); app notes 6:75 sub 0

## SUB 9 | Blood and Immunity Terms
[75:9:0] - **ABO blood groups** discovered by **Karl Landsteiner** (published **1901**).
[75:9:1] - Antigens sit on red blood cells; antibodies are in the plasma.
[75:9:2] TABLE head: Blood group | Antigen on RBC | Role
  [75:9:2.0] O negative | No A, no B, no Rh | Universal donor (red cells)
  [75:9:2.1] AB positive | A and B, Rh | Universal recipient
[75:9:3] - **Rh factor**: present = **Rh positive (+)**; absent = **Rh negative (-)**.
[75:9:4] - For plasma the universal donor is AB, not O.
[75:9:5] ~~ Sources: app notes 6:75 sub 9

[nfda123dfa18] sub7
  Which of the following pairs is correctly matched with the mission and its launch vehicle?
  *NISAR — GSLV-F16 | EOS-N1 — LVM3-M6 | BlueBird Block-2 — PSLV-C62 | CMS-03 — GSLV-F17
  x: NISAR was launched on GSLV-F16 (30 July 2025), so this pair is correct. EOS-N1 flew on PSLV-C62 and BlueBird Block-2 on LVM3-M6, so those pairs swap the vehicles. GSLV-F17 launched EOS-05 (ex-GISAT-1A), not CMS-03.
[nf966c3ac10b] sub7
  Which of the following is NOT correct about the 2026 criticism of ISRO?
  ISRO staff unions questioned outsourcing under the 2023 Space Policy | The criticism followed two consecutive PSLV failures | *The Department of Space abolished the 2023 Space Policy | About 100 ISRO staff quit the organisation
  x: The 2023 Space Policy was questioned after two PSLV failures in a row, but it was not abolished.
[na618da3ab54] sub7

  x: Vikram-1 is Skyroot Aerospace's privately developed orbital rocket, not an ISRO build.
[n5f4e91a8575] sub7

[f1aade00bec44] sub9
  Which blood group is the universal donor of red cells?
  AB positive | *O negative | O positive | AB negative
  x: O negative has no A, B or Rh antigen, so it is the universal red-cell donor; AB positive is the universal recipient.
[fde756fc1b9e1] sub9
  Consider the following statements about blood groups:
1. Antigens sit on red blood cells, while antibodies are in the plasma.
2. AB positive is the universal recipient.
3. For plasma, the universal donor is group O.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
  *1 and 2 only | 2 and 3 only | 1 and 3 only | 1, 2 and 3
  x: Statements 1 and 2 are correct; for plasma the universal donor is AB, not O.
[f77697a28a2df] sub9
  How is a person whose red blood cells carry the Rh factor classified?
  Rh negative | Group O | *Rh positive | Group AB
  x: Rh factor present means Rh positive; absent means Rh negative.

[82:3:2] # Symbols and Snakes
[82:3:3] - **National fish:** books and exam keys give the **Indian Mackerel**; no national fish has been formally notified. National **aquatic animal** = **Ganges river dolphin** (2009). Hilsa = national fish of Bangladesh.
[82:3:4] - **Viper bite:** **haemotoxic** venom (tissue and blood damage) – local necrosis, ecchymosis, blistering, painful swelling, compartment syndrome; **Russell's viper** causes more snakebite deaths than any other Indian snake. Sea snakes are mainly neurotoxic/myotoxic; the rat snake is non-venomous.
[82:3:5] - **Snow leopard:** declared **Kyrgyzstan's national symbol** (Dec 2023); Kyrgyzstan hosts the **GSLEP**; IUCN **Vulnerable**.
[82:3:6] # Big Mammals and Turtles
[82:3:7] - **Kaziranga (rhino):** the SC's **Central Empowered Committee (CEC)** pulled up **Assam** over **mining near the park** (Aug 2026).
[82:3:8] - **Olive Ridley:** **arribada** at **Gahirmatha and Rushikulya (Odisha)**; threat = **coastal erosion** of nesting beaches (Aug 2026).

[82:10:5] - **Seshachalam** Biosphere Reserve – **2010** (AP's first BR); **Koundinya** – AP's **only Elephant Reserve**.
[82:10:6] - **Tirupati** – in line for **Wetland City** status; **Guntur** – 2nd in Swachh Vayu 2026; AP top in SEEI 2024; **2nd in MISHTI** (not 1st).
[82:10:7] > 13 = BRs in the UNESCO network; 18 = all BRs in India.
[82:10:8] ~~ Sources: Rocket CA 2026 Part 02 p.137 (elephant reserves 33; Ramsar 103); Rocket Environment p.2 (Seshachalam = AP's first BR); RUNBOOK settled facts (58 tiger reserves); app notes 6:82 sub 6

## SUB 2 | Development and Hunger Frameworks
[89:2:0] - **HDI**: conceived by **Mahbub ul Haq** with **Amartya Sen**; UNDP HDR since **1990**; income = **GNI per capita (PPP)**; dimensions combined by a **geometric mean**.
[89:2:1] - **HDR 2025**: **Sri Lanka** (high human development) is the only offered neighbour well above India (130th, medium); Bangladesh, Nepal, Pakistan rank lower.
[89:2:2] - **GHI**: undernourishment and under-5 mortality **1/3** each; stunting and wasting **1/6** each.
[89:2:3] - **Human Capital Index (World Bank)**: **survival, quality-adjusted schooling, health** (not GNI).
[89:2:4] ~~ Sources: Rocket CA 2026 Part 02 p.85 (HDR 2025 India 130); app notes 6:89 (sub 2)
## SUB 3 | Economy and Flagship Reports
[89:3:0] - **UNCTAD TDR 2017**: world economy "**picking up but not lifting off**"; growth ~2.6% vs pre-2008 average 3.2%; **China and India unlikely to be global "growth poles"** at current growth.
[89:3:1] - **UNCTAD TDR 2026** ("The Geoeconomics of Development", Oct 2026): India **7.3%** in 2026.
[89:3:2] - **World Bank GEP (June 2026)**: India FY27 **6.6%**.
[89:3:3] - **WDR 2025**: "Standards for Development"; **WDR 2026**: "**The Promise of AI**".
[89:3:4] - **IMF WEO (Oct 2025)**: India 6.6% (2025-26).
[89:3:5] - **Largest economies** (nominal GDP): **USA, China (2nd)**; Japan was 2nd until 2010.
[89:3:6] - **Defence spending**: SIPRI 2016 fact sheet – India (~$51 bn) about **one-fourth** of China (~$215 bn); **IHS Jane's (Dec 2016)** – India **4th** in 2016 (after USA, China, UK).
[89:3:7] - **Forbes most powerful people**: Narendra Modi **9th** (2016, 2018).
[89:3:8] - **CDP 2019 climate A List**: **Japan** had the most companies (38), ahead of the USA.

  *1 only | 2 only | Both 1 and 2 | Neither 1 nor 2
  x: Statement 1 is correct: the HDI uses GNI per capita (PPP). Statement 2 is wrong: the HDI combines its dimension indices using a geometric mean, not an arithmetic mean. So 1 only.

[109:0:0] # January – May

  [109:0:1.0] National Youth Day | 12 January | Swami Vivekananda's birth anniversary (not International Youth Day)
  [109:0:1.1] Army Day | 15 January | 15 January 1949: K.M. Cariappa became the first Indian Commander-in-Chief, succeeding Sir Francis Roy Bucher
  [109:0:1.2] National Girl Child Day | 24 January | Since 2008, Ministry of Women and Child Development
  [109:0:1.3] National Voters' Day | 25 January | ECI foundation day (1950)
  [109:0:1.4] National Women's Day | 13 February | Birth anniversary of Sarojini Naidu (b. 13 Feb 1879; 2014 = 135th)
  [109:0:1.5] National Safety Day / Week | 4 March / 4–10 March | Founding of the National Safety Council (1966)
  [109:0:1.6] National Survey Day | 10 April | Survey of India founded 1767
  [109:0:1.7] Civil Services Day | 21 April | Patel's 1947 address at Metcalfe House, Delhi ("steel frame"); 18th in 2026
  [109:0:1.8] National Technology Day | 11 May | Pokhran-II (1998); 2017 theme "Technology for Inclusive and Sustainable Growth"
  [109:0:1.9] Anti-Terrorism Day | 21 May | Assassination of Rajiv Gandhi (1991) – not 11 May

[109:0:3] - **Republic Day chief guest 2016:** French President **François Hollande** (not Obama – 2015, not Cameron or Abe).

## SUB 13 | Publishing, Media, Language and Famous Quotes
[109:13:0] # Publishing and library terms
[109:13:1] TABLE head: Term | Meaning
  [109:13:1.0] ISBN | International Standard Book Number – for books (serials use ISSN)
  [109:13:1.1] INB | Indian National Bibliography (Central Reference Library, Kolkata)
  [109:13:1.2] FAIFE | IFLA's Freedom of Access to Information and Freedom of Expression
  [109:13:1.3] Paperback | Low-priced book with flexible paper covers
  [109:13:1.4] Review / Bibliography / Treatise / Textbook | Survey of primary literature / organised list of sources / comprehensive compilation / builds understanding → a-IV, b-III, c-I, d-II
  [109:13:1.5] MBO | Management by Objectives (Peter Drucker)
[109:13:2] # Media firsts
[109:13:3] - **Manorama Yearbook** – published by **Malayala Manorama**, Kottayam.
[109:13:4] - **Vividh Bharati** (AIR, 1957) – started to counter **Radio Ceylon**.
[109:13:5] - **Arun Bhoomi** – first Hindi daily of Arunachal Pradesh (November 2019).
[109:13:6] - **Titanic** printed its own daily, the **Atlantic Daily Bulletin**.
[109:13:7] - **Arunachal Pradesh** Assembly logo (January 2020): National Emblem + **Foxtail Orchid** (state flower); India's national flower = **Lotus**.
[109:13:8] - **Rishikesh** = "Yoga Capital of the World".
[109:13:9] # Language
[109:13:10] - **Spanish alphabet: 27 letters** (26 + ñ).
[109:13:11] - "**They**" (singular) – American Dialect Society's **word of the decade** (January 2020).
[109:13:12] - Idiom "**by the skin of your teeth**" = **just barely**; **soliloquy** = speaking one's thoughts aloud alone, especially by a character in a play (sinecure = paid post with no work).
[109:13:13] # Who said it
[109:13:14] TABLE head: Quote | Author
  [109:13:14.0] "Knowledge is power" | Francis Bacon
  [109:13:14.1] "Where is the wisdom we have lost in knowledge?" | T.S. Eliot (The Rock, 1934)
  [109:13:14.2] "Merely to stuff the child with information … the most unintelligent form of education" | J. Krishnamurti

## SUB 14 | Observance Days – Chronology (Revision)
[109:14:0] TABLE head: Month | Days in order
  [109:14:0.0] January | Youth Day 12 Jan → Army Day 15 Jan → Girl Child Day and Education Day 24 Jan → Voters' Day 25 Jan → Republic Day 26 Jan
  [109:14:0.1] April–May | Survey Day 10 Apr → Civil Services Day 21 Apr → Earth Day 22 Apr → Labour Day 1 May → Technology Day 11 May → Nurses Day 12 May → Anti-Terrorism Day 21 May
  [109:14:0.2] June | Milk Day 1 Jun → Environment Day 5 Jun → Oceans Day 8 Jun → Desertification Day 17 Jun → Yoga Day 21 Jun → AIFF Grassroots Day 23 Jun → Samvidhan Hatya Diwas 25 Jun → Tropics 29 Jun → Parliamentarism 30 Jun
  [109:14:0.3] August | Handloom Day 7 Aug → Library Day 12 Aug → Space Day 23 Aug → Sports Day 29 Aug
  [109:14:0.4] September | Teachers' Day 5 Sep → Clean Air Day 7 Sep → Literacy Day 8 Sep → Hindi Diwas 14 Sep → Ayurveda Day and Sign Languages Day 23 Sep
  [109:14:0.5] October–December | Postal Day 10 Oct → Disaster Risk Reduction Day 13 Oct → Students' Day 15 Oct → Statistics Day 20 Oct → Ekta Diwas 31 Oct → Diabetes Day 14 Nov → Janjatiya Gaurav 15 Nov → Toilet Day 19 Nov → Constitution Day 26 Nov → AIDS Day 1 Dec → Disability Day 3 Dec → Volunteer and Soil Day 5 Dec → Submarine Day 8 Dec → Kisan Diwas 23 Dec → Consumer Day 24 Dec → Good Governance Day 25 Dec → Veer Bal Diwas 26 Dec
[109:14:1] # Days declared by other bodies
[109:14:2] TABLE head: Day / week | Date | Body / note
  [109:14:2.0] AIFF Grassroots Day | 23 June | Declared by the All India Football Federation (2023); birth anniversary of P.K. Banerjee
  [109:14:2.1] AFC Grassroots Football Day | 15 May | Asian Football Confederation
  [109:14:2.2] Vigilance Awareness Week 2025 | 27 October – 2 November 2025 | CVC; theme "Vigilance: Our Shared Responsibility"; week of Patel's birth anniversary
  [109:14:2.3] Rashtriya Poshan Maah | Every September | Ministry of Women and Child Development (Poshan Abhiyaan, 8 March 2018, Jhunjhunu); 8th launched 17 September 2025
[109:14:3] - Calendar order trap: **Library Day → Sports Day → Teachers' Day → Hindi Diwas**; between Yoga Day (21 Jun) and Samvidhan Hatya Diwas (25 Jun) falls **AIFF Grassroots Day (23 Jun)**.
[109:14:4] - AP observes Yoga Day, Youth Day and the Day against Drug Abuse in all districts (2024-25).
